It doesn't know about totals.\n\nAlternatively, maybe export_csv should call render_rows on the original rows, and if totals=True, it should also call render_rows on the totals row (computed via column_totals) and append.\n\nLet's look at the test test_export_without_totals: assert export_csv([[1, 2], [3, 4]]) == \"1,2\\n3,4\". Currently render_rows(rows) returns list of strings for each row. If we pass totals_row as a row, render_rows will render it as \"TOTAL,4,6\" if totals_row is [\"TOTAL\", 4, 6]. If we call render_rows([total_row]), it returns [rendered_total_row_string]. We need to append that string.\n\nAlternatively, we could modify render_rows to handle totals row differently, but let's keep it simple.\n\nWe need to check if render_rows expects to receive a row with mixed types (string and numbers).
